### Settled

**Incarnation identity.** A `resuming` incarnation is the `coordination_generation` value its `resume_pending → resuming` write increments to; every non-terminal exit is conditioned on `resuming` at that value (SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**). Rejected: state plus unchanged generation (ABA), `UpdatedAt` token, `recovery_generation`, a new column.
**Deleted SPEC-1b claims.** "At most one attempt per session at a time" and "an attempt ends by the `resuming` watchdog deadline" are deleted.
**`sweepResuming` exit.** Guarded on state plus the listed row's generation; `errResumingSuperseded` on a miss (CODE-4 item 15).
**Entry CAS.** The entry write increments under CAS on the value read before the lease was confirmed.
**Single gateway releaser.** The replacement pod is in neither `PodAssignment` nor the registry before the commit (CODE-4 item 4).
**Orphan-claim GC.** Reclaims a stopped attempt's pod; draining a live attempt older than `claimOrphanTimeout` is accepted in SPEC-1b; `Binder.Resume` claims a fresh idle pod, so the statement holds on slot pools too.
**No post-exit bump.** The post-exit call is deleted (failure.go:319 by CODE-4 item 3.7; resume.go:91 goes with the `handleResume` rewrite); the mid-resume terminal bump is in the terminal write (D7 **Mark.**), and the function is deleted ([non-spec.45.review-citations.1]).
**Generation incrementers.** Takeover CAS (§10.1.2 step 1), resume entry (SPEC-1b), mid-resume terminal collapse (§7.2 step 4(a)); §10.1.1 (SPEC-1f item 2) lists the first two; the terminal bump stays in §7.2.
**§4.2 pointer.** SPEC-1f item 1: field description plus bare pointer "See §10.1.1 **Generation counters:**."
**Never-advanced stamp.** SPEC-1f item 2 keeps the baseline clause, reworded to "a session that no takeover and no resume driver write has advanced".
**§10.1.5 trigger.** Only a pod's generation-stale rejection triggers §10.1.5 (SPEC-1f items 3, 4).
**§10.1.5 no-pod paragraph.** SPEC-1f item 3 adds **Session with no bound pod.**; its residual sentence on stale pod releases is gone (generation scope, see **Pod-release lease scope.**).
**Sweeper generation.** Compares the row with the binding's stamp; `RecordHandoff` is a CAS on the listed row's generation and checks `adoptedState` on the locked row (CODE-5 items 2a, 2b); `renewedGeneration` deleted.
**Adoption read.** SPEC-2b widens §10.1.2 step 0 to read `state` and `pod_assignment`; §29.8 step 4 cites step 0.
**`resuming` not adopted.** Every replica evaluates the §6.2 `resuming` watchdog from the row.
**Lease retention.** SPEC-2a is the one home: renewal while a pod is bound or driver work is pending (`starting`, `running`, `input_required`, held-pod `suspended`, `resume_pending`, `resuming`) and in `awaiting_client_action` (0091 retention, 900 s cap); podless `suspended`, `created`, `finalizing`, `ready` hold none; adoption covers `resume_pending`, `awaiting_client_action`, and any `running`, `input_required`, or `suspended` row that names a pod.
**Podless §29.3 rows.** Every route a podless `suspended` session admits has a non-coordinator server: messages (SPEC-5l), resume (SPEC-3j), terminate and DELETE (SPEC-2d item 2; DELETE inherits "the same states"), resolution (SPEC-2d item 3), events JSON and streaming (relay, SPEC-2d items 4, 5), logs (EventStore); interrupt and upload admit `running` only. No lease code is owed: terminate, delete, and resolution handlers read no lease.
**SPEC-2d item 5.** The §15.1 events-endpoint cell names the `CH-EVENTRELAY` backlog (resolves OD-P4); RECORDS-1 7(o) files the §10.4 reattach buffer only.
**Terminate state cell.** SPEC-2d item 2's cell is the intersection of §15.1 terminate preconditions (no `input_required`, no `resuming`) with SPEC-2a; it is not a copy.
**Sweeper mirror release.** The Sweeper releases a podless row's mirror on each sweep while it is the prior holder (CODE-5 item 1, OD-P1); the skip sits before `eligible` and before the dead-connection branch, after `leases.Get` (it needs `priorHolder`).
**Readopt binding.** `readoptAndFence` resolves the pool pinned to `row.PoolRef` and sets `SlotID = sessionID` when `MaxConcurrentSessions > 1`, plus `Recycle`, `CleanupCommands`, `CleanupTimeoutSeconds` (CODE-5 item 2).
**No on-demand adoption.** None exists; no podless forward exists.
**Fence-skip home.** §10.1.2 step 2 (SPEC-2b) is the one home of the podless skip; steps 0 and 1 still run.
**SPEC-2c sites.** Fence universals become §10.1.2 citations (§28.5.1 CH-ATTACH and CH-FENCE, §28.6, §28.8 rows, §29.4 step 4, §29.5 step 3, §4.7.1 `CoordinatorFence` row, CH-CHECKPOINT/CH-BARRIER Preconditions); item 2 makes CH-FENCE **Exclusivity.** "announced to a bound pod by this channel"; item 8 makes the §28.8 CH-FENCE row "announces the holder change to a bound pod (§10.1.2 step 2)".
**SPEC-1a2.** The §28.5.1 CH-ATTACH **Degradation.** bullet reads "is restored onto a replacement pod by the resume driver, or reaches `awaiting_client_action` when its `maxResumeWindowSeconds` cap elapses ([§6.2] **`resume_pending` wall-clock cap:**, [§7.2], [§7.3])". The §6.2 cap paragraph (SPEC-1c2) is the one home of the window's stop rule. SPEC-1a step 3c, SPEC-1e items 7 and 8, SPEC-1h (b), SPEC-1a2, SPEC-3n, and DOCS-1a's `internal.md` bullet point at it or name the cap, and no other site restates when the timer stops.
**SPEC-1a step 3e.** Rebuild applies to "a session that has neither a checkpoint nor a §4.4.5 minimal state record"; SPEC-1c **Retry policy:** points at the §7.3 resume flow.
**No fence at bind.** The driver's writes are generation-conditioned; code fences after the commit (CODE-4 item 5.4).
**Pre-attach collapse.** SPEC-1e keys it on the `resume_pending → resuming` write.
**Post-claim failure reason.** SPEC-1b item 3 is the one home: `setup_command_failed` for a deterministic setup failure (gRPC `FailedPrecondition`), then `credential_rejected` for a refusal matching SPEC-1g's predicate, `runtime_crash` otherwise ([spec.30.fix-design-G2.1]); the §7.3 classifier (per-session overridable) picks the §6.2 edge. SPEC-3b, SPEC-3c, and the Edge-cases bullet only point at it.
**SPEC-1c relabel.** §6.2's first `resuming` bullet is "Retryable failure during `resuming`"; the non-retryable example is a setup-command failure during a snapshotless rebuild.
**Retry accounting.** SPEC-1b **Retry accounting.** spends a retry on a pod failure, a post-entry driver failure, or the watchdog; nothing else.
**Route list home.** The routes into recovery are listed once, in the **Resume driver.** opening.
**SPEC-3o.** A pointer at **Resume driver.** items 1 and 2.
**§29.6 podless resume.** Untraced entry point (SPEC-3k, SPEC-3m).
**Traversal home.** §8.10 owns delegation-tree traversal.
**SPEC-3b frame.** REST resume row names `status_change`; `state_change` is the adapter-facing kind.
**Held-pod resume.** No pod RPC; the coordinator writes `suspended → running` and delivers per path 6.
**Off-holder held-pod `/resume`.** Fails closed with `TARGET_NOT_READY`, details `currentState` alone (CODE-4 item 8); MCP `resume_session` proxies the same route.
**Owner answers.** OD-2 (Decision 20), OD-3 (21), OD-B immediate `resume_pending` (22), OD-R1/OD-C1 delivery at the pre-running commit (23), OD-1 watchdog bullet unchanged (anchor drift in **Watchdog anchor.**), OD-A synchronous `StampSync` (withdrawn, Decision 25), OD-R2 durable-inbox `EXPIRE` deleted. Later answers: OD-N1 to OD-N5 as Decisions 31 to 34 plus confirmations of 16 and 27 to 30; OD-R2 (delegated answers stand) yes and OD-R3 (local-development line) keep of [redesign1.apply.2]; OD-1 migration 0184 for CODE-7; OD-H1 A (Decision 36); OD-T1 A (Decision 21, grounded in the owner's OD-3 question text in c90f207c9); OD-T2 B and OD-H2 A under the 2026-10-09 delegation ([f9.human-decisions.OD-1], [f9.human-decisions.OD-R2], [f9.human-decisions.OD-R3], [f9.human-decisions.OD-H1], [f9.human-decisions.OD-T1], [redesign1.apply.5]).
**Redesign-6 resolutions.** OD-P3 as the gateway-leader backlog re-check; OD-C2 as §12.8 erasure (SPEC-5aa, Decision 24, CODE-6 item 17); OD-C3 as the generation-scoped pod release (SPEC-1g, SPEC-1g3, CODE-4 item 24). Decisions 16 and 27 to 30 record the 2026-10-10 answers; OD-I1 and OD-I3 resolved to B and OD-I2 and OD-I4 to A (see **`busy` scope.** and **Connector calls.**).
**`ReattachNode`.** Stops calling `resumeOnPod` (CODE-4 item 11).
**Custody home.** SPEC-5i path 6: synchronous delivery only for a REST message the coordinator serves while the row names a pod; every other message is held in the DLQ before any state write; a podless hold is followed by the guarded `resume_pending` write.
**Backlog resume.** SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** is the one home of the mechanisms: the coordinator's sweep for a held-pod row, the release's one check, and the gateway leader's re-check for podless rows (CODE-6 item 16, interval `gateway.suspendedBacklogRecheckSeconds` minimum 30 s, migration 0183 partial index `idx_sessions_podless_suspended`). The leader evaluates at once on Lease acquisition (`Run` calls `Pass` first), so failover delays it by at most the §12.5 window (25 s). Exclusivity is scoped to "that condition".
**Backlog predicate.** Counts the inbox only for an `Idle` suspension and otherwise only DLQ entries accepted after `SuspendedAt` (`HeldLen`, `CountAcceptedAfter`).
**Release-time inbox migration.** SPEC-5b step 2 order is guarded clear, migrate the in-memory inbox to the DLQ, release; §7.2 migration paragraph (SPEC-5z item 11) is the home of both triggers and the failure outcome; `MigrateInboxToDLQ` gains a state parameter (`session_state` label `suspended` at the release).
**Drain-failure rows.** SPEC-5q drops the `resume_pending` qualifier from §16.1 and §16.5; CODE-6 item 4 step 3 rewrites the `InboxDrainFailure` Description in rules.go and runs `make generate` (docs/alerting/rules.yaml, charts/lenny/files/alerting-rules.yaml, manifests.yaml).
**Adapter seal.** Homes: SPEC-5ab **Session seal.** owns the activity clock, ack attribution, the compare-and-seal, who lifts the seal, and how an unlifted seal ends; SPEC-5ac owns the per-entry `heartbeat` (from `session_start` to the session's `session_end` or entry removal, independent of `Attach`, OD-S8), `busy`, and the runtime MUST; SPEC-5f owns the gateway's reaction to each answer and the idle expiry of a session that cannot be idle-suspended (`type: mcp`, `executionMode: service`); §11.1 (SPEC-6a) owns the cap exclusion and the resume check. SPEC-5ab **Session seal.** also owns the activity definition and the case with no attributed busy (frames and platform and connector calls); do not restate either here. Nothing about activity crosses a checkpoint. Refusals: no `session_start` written; an untapped runtime (`activity_untapped:`). A not-sealed answer keeps an earlier seal. The idle suspension seals, checkpoints, writes `running → suspended` with the pod cleared, then releases. Only the attempt that placed the seal lifts it, on a checkpoint failure or a same-generation, same-pod write miss; an unlifted seal ends by a later attempt, entry removal, or `maxSessionAge`, never by a `CoordinatorFence`. CODE-6 items 4, 5, and 18 implement it. Redesign-7 OD-1 is withdrawn.
**Cross-replica slot release.** Refuted. A release clears the pod only after its checkpoint wrote `WorkspaceSnapshot` (pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:438-458). Every later restore therefore takes `Binder.Resume` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:37-46, :102), which claims an `Idle`-phase pod or rebinds a scrubbed reserved hold (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:157-166, :179), never the released session's pod. The releasing replica's unconditional `Shutdown` (pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go) reaches only the old pod's adapter.
**Held-message delivery.** SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.**: peek the older head, remove after `Send`, run at every commit to `running` and before every path-2 delivery, expire nothing; no at-least-once sentence.
**Held read failure.** An inbox or DLQ read failure is not a failed delivery: `deliverBuffered` logs WARN and returns nil (CODE-4 item 6), and the message in hand proceeds under its own path; the sentence sits after the failed-delivery consequences.
**Failed held delivery.** The replica stops and the entry stays at the head.
**Terminal race.** `holdMessage` re-reads with `ReadPrimary` after the enqueue and removes its own entries only on a terminal or failed re-read.
**Receipt home.** **Held messages.** plus **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.**
**§15.4 `delivery` table.** SPEC-5k's sentence below the table overrides the `"queued"` row for `suspended`; do not delete it.
**`inbox_unavailable`.** SPEC-5z item 9 widens it to DLQ enqueue failures and a failed re-read.
**DLQ TTL home.** Last sentence of **Delivery of held messages.**: the TTL of a DLQ entry and of a durable-inbox entry runs from the later of acceptance and entry to the current recovering state, enforced only in recovering states; code gates `sweepDLQExpiry` on `now - row.UpdatedAt >= DefaultDLQTTL` (CODE-6 item 12); SPEC-5z item 4 points the Per-message TTL row there and deletes its activation sentence.
**DLQ scores.** Score is `EnqueuedAt + DefaultDLQTTL` (acceptance order, SPEC-5z items 3 and 12), and the score is no longer the expiry.
**Reason definitions.** §15.4 is the only home.
**Terminal drain.** SPEC-5z item 1 covers every DLQ entry; `DrainOnTerminal` already drains inbox and DLQ.
**`inbox_cleared`.** SPEC-5z item 13 fires it only on adoption where step 0 finds `running`, `input_required`, or held-pod `suspended`; RECORDS-1 7(h) files the emission.
**§29.4 step 9.** SPEC-5y wording; step 9 still traces an interrupt suspension.
**Resume before release.** SPEC-5b carries "unless it resumes the session for held messages"; CODE-6 item 2 runs the backlog resume before the release in the same tick.
**Pod-named condition.** Stated once, in the path 6 lead; code: `resumeHeldPod` guard and `errResumeHeldPodReleased`.
**`send_message` receipt.** `queued` on both branches for a `suspended` target; only a coordinator-served REST message to a held pod gets `delivered`.
**`clearInboxOnResume`.** Deleted.
**No `suspended` forward.** None exists.
**SPEC-5i2 pointers.** Items 1, 3, 4 as staged.
**One payload encoding.** MCP marshals `sessionserver.MessagePayload`; `deliverBuffered` decodes only that.
**Held-delivery context.** Server-lifetime context per entry (CODE-4 item 6).
**PreMessageDelivery.** MCP block moves ahead of `Classify`; `deliverBuffered` runs no interceptor.
**Held REJECT.** Audit row, no publish, remove, continue; audit failure stops the loop.
**PostAgentOutput signature.** As CODE-4 states; `route.go` is a CODE-4 target.
**Refused delivery.** SPEC-5f: a refused held message stays held; others are re-routed by current state.
**Idle atomicity.** The seal and the generation together are the atomicity witness. The suspend write takes effect only while the row is `running`, names the pod, has the listed `LastAgentActivityAt`, and carries `bind.CoordinationGeneration`, and it clears the pod. `classifyIdleWrite` decides every try of that write, first or repeated after a store error, on the locked row alone (CODE-6 item 4 step 3). Rejected: a list of post-error row forms, a `SuspendedAt` identity, and a new column.
**Admission fail-open.** Withdrawn by redesign 9. No admission write exists, so a delivery during a §12.3 failover needs no fail-open, and a stale-row delivery to a sealed session is refused at the adapter.
**Re-route read.** After `executor.ErrSessionSealed` the re-route of the undelivered messages reads the primary (CODE-6 item 7); the first message-path read classifies through `ReadPrimary` under the delivery lock.
**Idle rule home.** SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**
**Idle clock reset.** Stated once in the §6.2 intro; excludes `input_required`.
**`"idle"` trigger.** SPEC-5x deletes it; carriers in CODE-6 item 13.
**Docs mirrors.** DOCS-1b mirrors SPEC-5w; SPEC-5y has none.
**SPEC-5d2.** Deletes the `maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` glosses.
**Release guard.** `State == suspended && SuspendedAt.Equal(...) && PodAssignment == ...` (CODE-6 item 4.3).
**Suspension release step 4.** Releases through `releaseReportedBinding(ctx, row.ID, bind, DispositionCompleted)` on the `*BindResult` the binding check read, under `slotAccountLocks`; pointer identity discriminates; the helper gains a disposition parameter and a bool result; a skip emits nothing.
**Budget keys.** SPEC-5o re-arms every key of the root's tree on entry to and exit from `suspended` and when the row leaves `awaiting_client_action` for `resume_pending`; TTL 691200 s.
**Budget-key carrier.** CODE-6 item 14: `RearmTree(ctx, root, parentIDs)` pipelines EXPIRE on named keys with no SCAN; the server lists `ListByRoot` by effective root (`RootSessionID` else `ID`) and passes every listed row's `ParentSessionID`; flag `--delegation-budget-key-ttl-seconds`, chart `delegation.budgetKeyTTLSeconds`, startup refusal when TTL does not exceed the suspended lifetime; `PurgeRoot` keeps its Cluster routing fix (`TestPurgeRootRoutesToHashTagOwner`).
**Interrupt single writer.** MCP tool proxies `/interrupt` (CODE-6 item 1); `handleInterrupt` returns `session.Validate(EndpointInterrupt, r.State)` inside its mutator, answered by `writePreconditionError`.
**Wiring helpers.** `budgetKeyRearmer`, pinned by `TestBudgetRearmerWiring`. `wireTurnAdmitter` is deleted with the admission write.
**Suspended expiry miss.** `sweepSuspended` returns `errSuspendedSuperseded`.
**Session-row read authority.** Non-spec Design **Session-row read authority.** is the one home (rules R1 to R3); `sessionstore.ReadPrimary` is the one primary read; declining mutators return sentinels (`errMaxAgeSuperseded`, `errResumePendingSuperseded`, `errOrphanSuperseded`,  `errIdleWriteNotApplied`); `errHoldReread` is deleted; RECORDS-1 7(k) files the remaining nil-returning mutators.
**Message-path read failures.** Non-spec Design **Message-path read failures.** is the one home for the session-row reads on the message paths (invariants I1 and I2, one table row per read), and it cites CODE-4 items 6 and 7 and CODE-6 items 2, 4, and 16.3 for the other reads. Only the classification read falls back to the entry row. The re-read after a seal refusal and the `bufferAfterResumedSendFailure` re-read read the primary only and take the held branch on any error, and the inbox-on-error branch is deleted. `DeliverHeld` and `ResumeSuspended` take their caller's row and read none, and the delegate_task handler reads the child from the primary after the task input before it calls `DeliverHeld`, which supersedes the ledger DECISIONs that gave `DeliverHeld` a read-pool fallback. A path-2 refusal re-routes once. Do not add a helper that returns a row with its read source.
**Orphan reconciler guard.** `failOrphan`'s mutator writes only when `eligible(*s) && s.PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment` (CODE-6 item 4); it cites §10.1.4.
**Funnel suspended guard.** From a `suspended` source each funnel mutator returns `errReportConflict` unless `r.PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment && s.holdsPod(*r)` (CODE-4 item 22); `TestFailureReportOnReleasedSuspendedRowIsNoOp` is table-driven over the three mutators with admit control rows.
**Design lease gate.** Admits every session when no lease store or no pod binder is wired (Compose Mode); off-holder tests name "a pod binder wired", and release/backlog cases also a registry binding.
**`HeldLen` error.** `ResumeBacklog` logs and returns true; the release's check logs and continues.
**`ReleaseSuspendedPod`.** Off `Tick`, per-replica in-flight set, 60 s checkpoint deadline, context-aware `lockFlight`.
**Idle default fallback.** CODE-6 item 9 deletes the age-cap fallback.
**Release after the claim.** CODE-3 splits `connect` into claim and handshake halves with the hook between; a hook error or post-hook handshake failure releases the claim (`DeleteClaim` on `Claim`/`Bind`, `releaseResumeSlot(leaked=false)` on `Resume`); the nil-hook `Claim` release is unconditional; `Bind` releases only `MintedLeaseIDs`.
**Lost-commit release.** `Binder.ReleaseUncommitted` releases the attempt's own leases.
**Resume credentials.** Option A: the suspension release returns leases; every restore assigns new leases after the hook and handshake and before `cl.Resume` (SPEC-1g, CODE-4 item 20).
**Lease generation.** SPEC-1g: "Each credential lease records a generation." A restore's leases record the generation its entry write set; other leases record 0; a renewal or rotation records the replaced lease's value.
**Pod-release lease scope.** A pod release returns only leases at or below its binding stamp (CODE-4 item 24, `ReleaseThrough`); only `failPhase`, `ReclaimClaimed`, and `revokeFinalizeLease` keep the session-wide release.
**Renewal stop.** CODE-4 item 23: `Renew` refuses with `ErrLeaseReleased` when `LeaseStore.Lookup` finds no record or no pool binding, re-reads after `Assign`, both `ReleaseSession` implementations list twice; `onExtend` advances rows through update-only `Advance`; SPEC-1g3 edits §4.9 renewal step 1.
**Funnel lock.** CODE-4 item 22 keeps `slotAccountLocks` so a same-replica driver's replacement binding is not torn down by the funnel's `releaseExecutor`.
**Single lease acquire.** CODE-4 item 3.3; `publishBinding` on the rebuild branch.
**Release before rebind.** `releaseThenResumeOnPod(ctx, row, onClaimed)`.
**`completeResume` context.** Server-lifetime context.
**Commit repeat.** Item 5.2 repeats on a store error other than `errResumeCommitMissed` and `sessionstore.ErrNotFound`, so a hard-deleted row ends the loop.
**Commit-miss classification.** Home: item 5.3 `classifyCommit` and its table.
**Same-replica cancel.** Home: CODE-4 item 2a; SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.** states the outcome; CODE-3 item 3 **Detached release.** keeps the claim release working on a cancelled context.
**`bumpRecoveryGeneration`.** Deleted by CODE-4 item 4.
**`holdsPod`.** Defined in CODE-4 item 8 (`PodAssignment != ""` or no binder).
**Binderless driver.** `DriveResume` runs `completeResume` with no binder.
**Over-cap skip.** Lives only in CODE-4 item 1.
**Test landing rule.** A test listed under a code deliverable lands in that step; it may assert only behavior that step or an earlier one builds, and it goes under the step that creates the newest symbol it calls.
**TEST-0.** Test-lane step at S7, ahead of the S8 schema step.
**Migration 0182 test.** Tier 2 `prodMigrationSchema` entry plus CHECK assertion.
**Migration 0183 test.** `{migration: "0183", table: "sessions"}` entry plus `mustHaveIndex`/`mustNotHaveIndex`.
**Migration 0184 test.** `{migration: "0184", table: "sessions"}` entry plus `mustHaveIndex`/`mustNotHaveIndex` for `idx_sessions_counted_by_tenant` and `idx_sessions_active_by_tenant`, both directions (CODE-7).
**CODE-4 tiers.** S14 (CODE-4) declares tier 2 for the `credleasestore` `Lookup`/`Advance` test.
**`TestStaleReplicaReleaseKeepsPeerRestoreLeases_spec_10_1_5`.** Under CODE-6.
**`TestStartCommitDeliversPreRunningHeld`.** Under CODE-4, asserting redelivery through a later `deliverBuffered`.
**spec-map rule.** The step that adds a test at tier 2 or above maps it in `tests/spec-map.json`, and the step that deletes or renames a test at any tier updates its entries; the rule's one home is the `## Testing` intro ([non-spec.25.fix-G3.1], [non-spec.25.fix-design-G3.1]).
**Test homes.** As the Testing section states; CODE-1 accrual test visits `starting` and `input_required`.
**Expiry hook test.** Rewritten onto a suspended row.
**Checklist.** S1 to S6 point at SPEC-1 to SPEC-6, each reading "Lands every SPEC-N edit" so sub-items such as SPEC-1g3 and SPEC-5p2 are covered ([reconcile.deliverables.9]); S7 is TEST-0; S8 lands the **Heartbeat frames.** schema edits; S9 to S19 are DOCS-1a, CODE-2, CODE-3, CODE-5, CODE-1, CODE-4, CODE-6, CODE-7, DOCS-1b, TEST-1, and RECORDS-1.
**Chart.** CODE-6 item 11 edits values.yaml, and also rewrites the `MaxSessionMinutes` `desc` tag in `pkg/chart/values/values.go` and regenerates `charts/lenny/values.schema.json` for the active-time cap (OD-S7); `gateway` and `delegation` are open Objects, so the new values need no schema edit.
**openapi descriptions.** Age wording in CODE-1 item 4, idle wording in CODE-6 item 9; `generated_schemas.go` comes from `go generate` in mcptools, outside `make generate`; the `/resume` summary stays unstaged.
**Age docs and help.** As CODE-1 and DOCS-1b name.
**Client-guide targets.** DOCS-1b names them item by item, including the `input_required` timer sentence and the `/resume` preconditions row (DOCS-1a).
**DOCS-1b credential pages.** concepts.md **Credential routing** and **Pool exhaustion**, the credential-pool-exhaustion runbook (frontmatter and Trigger list), and mcp-sdk.md walkthroughs.
**`session_expired` rows.** DOCS-1b rewrites only the `maxClientIdleSeconds` half, so that it covers the idle expiry of a session that cannot be idle-suspended and the suspended-session lifetime expiry; `maxSessionAge` stays. SPEC-5r names all three causes on the §14 line.
**runtime-configuration.md.** DOCS-1b deletes `limits.maxIdleTimeSeconds`.
**Proposal shape.** One proposal.
**Impact rows.** 0077 Draft; 0079 nothing; 0081 Implemented, SPEC-3g edits its envelope; 0084 Draft, the row names the `Server` struct (pkg/adapter/server.go) and `platformToolProvider.Call` collisions, `runtimeSession` as the resolver the second lander uses, and the `connectorToolProvider.Call` collision under OD-I3 B, and drops the `sessionseal.go` sentence ([f7.apply-0084-impact]; the shared `resume.go` and proto overlap was refuted, [f2.cleanup]); 0085 Draft, rebase; 0086 Draft, mcp-sdk.md and `postByID` overlap; 0087 Draft, §4.7.1 and pkg/adapter rebase only, no row ([non-spec.13.review-edit-sites.1]); 0091 retired hold; 0090 row as the baseline wrote it.
**0091 lease behavior.** 0091 changed no REG-COORDLEASE write.
**Budget keys in the tree.** `DefaultTTL` 24 h set only when `TTL == -1`; missing key reads as 0; no settle-time deletion; tree keys are tree_size, tree_memory, tokens, parallel_children:{p}, children_total:{p}, parallel_children_hwm, token_grant.
**`resume_pending` keeps the pod name.** "No bound pod" is read from state.
**Send and context.** `Send` returns the ctx error mid-turn after consumption.
**Gateway-caused stream ends.** `closedByGateway` skips the failure handler; no deadlock on `slotAccountLocks`.
**Checkpoint deadline.** The production checkpointer sets none.
**Commit witness.** `pgstore.Update` can error after a landed COMMIT.
**Replica reads.** `pgstore.Get`, `GetByID`, `List`, `ListByRoot` read `s.read`; `Update` locks on the primary; a mutator error writes nothing; a nil mutator return writes and advances `UpdatedAt`.
**Suspension metrics.** CODE-6 item 4 is the first emitter.
**Anchors and sweeps.** Every staged anchor resolves once with whitespace collapsed; identifier sweeps find every site staged. Do not re-audit without a new diff.
**Plan scope.** The single §10.2 item (plan:2474-2525); F-7.3.31 is not assigned; RECORDS-1 items 3 to 6 dispose of 0091's filings; the expired-pod recycle is declined (item 9).
**Kubernetes surface.** No CRD field, status write, finalizer, webhook, or request-path reconcile; claim DELETEs reuse `podclaim.DeleteClaim`; the leader job uses the existing `lenny-gateway-leader` Lease.
**Lease timing.** Sweeper interval 15 s, lease TTL 60 s; replica IDs carry a random suffix, so a restart never matches `priorHolder`; the Postgres lease fallback lapses on TTL.
**DLQ key.** No key-level EXPIRE exists.
**Leader capacity.** The caps do not bound the podless `suspended` population: SPEC-6a exempts `suspended` from every cap, and the per-user cap defaults to 0, disabled (§8.10 Delegation Tree Recovery, the detached-orphan **Note:**). The bound is creation rate × (`gateway.maxSuspendedSessionSeconds` 604800 s + one idle window); at Tier 3 (200 creations/s, §16.5 Alerting Rules and SLOs, the session-creation-rate row) that is about 1.2M rows per 1% of creations that end idle. A leader pass lists them through `idx_sessions_podless_suspended` (0183, `(tenant_id, id)`) in keyset pages of 500, holding one page at a time, and issues one `HeldLen` per row per 300 s with at most 32 in flight (CODE-6 items 16.2 and 16.3); at the ~6.65M-row worst case that needs a mean `HeldLen` round trip under about 1.4 ms ([non-spec.25.fix-G2.1]), plus `admitResume` COUNTs only for rows holding messages while a cap is enabled; each COUNT reads `idx_sessions_counted_by_tenant` (0184), which holds no `suspended` row. The pre-existing per-replica full scans (7(l)) dominate it ([spec.17.review-performance.1], [spec.21.review-performance.1]).
**Refuted: second slot for one session ID.** The adapter registry is `map[string]*slotState` keyed by session ID (pkg/adapter/server.go:388), and a claim on a started entry is refused unless it is the idempotent repeat (pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:109-114).
**Refuted: re-emitted `toolCallId` reuses an approval.** `AwaitApproval` re-records the interaction as `PhasePending` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/toolapproval.go:106-116). The pgstore overwrites on the same key (interactionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:73-75, `phase = EXCLUDED.phase`), the memory store replaces the entry (interactionstore.go:141-152), and the waiter registry keeps no verdict after `Resolve` (toolapproval/toolapproval.go:83-95).
**Podless terminate racing a restore.** Confirmed in `handleTransition`: its mutator wrote `completed` over a read-pool `Get` without re-validation (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_transitions.go:63-83), which could complete a `resuming` row that `EndpointTerminate` refuses (pkg/api/v1/session/session.go:297-305). The fix is staged in CODE-6 item 1.
**`AdapterTerminating`.** No gateway code consumes it. The adapter emits it (pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:356), and the gateway names it only in a comment (pkg/gateway/session/orphansession/orphansession.go:9). Before the commit only the attempt holds the replacement pod's connection, so the §10.1.4 transition is the attempt's own failure report and takes the `ExpectedCoordinationGeneration` guard when a consumer is built. Not filed.
**Rule-2 attribution.** Superseded: Design D4 states the listing rule as one invariant and lists no reconcilers ([restructure.non-spec-to-constraints]).
**Refuted: Postgres lease fallback holds a transaction for the lease life.** The fallback takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` per operation and stores the holder and `expires_at` in `session_leases` (pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:9-17, 63-65). The `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` wording in §10.1.1 **Per-session coordination:** predates 0092, and this proposal does not file it.
**§27.6 `idle timeout`.** It means the playground login-record idle sweep (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/auth.go:321-343). That sweep is wired at cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:494-501, which states that the agent-session idle reaper does not reclaim these records. Idle suspension leaves it unchanged.
**Cap exemption.** Decision 27: a `suspended` session, with or without its pod, counts toward no concurrent-session cap. The held-pod half rests on the owner's 2026-10-10 words, "Suspended sessions should not count towards the max number of concurrent sessions", which name no pod condition (OD-S10). Every `suspended → running`/`resume_pending` writer (path-6 lead podless fallback, synchronous delivery, held messages, coordinator backlog sweep, releasing replica, leader re-check, `/resume`, `resume_session`) passes SPEC-6a **Resume admission.**; the failure edge is the only unchecked exit, and SPEC-6a states it (OD-S9). The counts are primary-pool Postgres COUNTs that fail closed with 500 and need no Redis fallback (quota.go). A `429` resume is cached per idempotency key for 24 h, so SPEC-6a's "retry with a new key" holds.
**Resume-admission home.** SPEC-6a **Resume admission.** is the single statement of a refusal's outcome; SPEC-3b, SPEC-3j, SPEC-5i **Admission.**, and SPEC-5j cite it, and SPEC-6d, SPEC-3e, SPEC-5f, and SPEC-5l carry citations only; the inventory still held after redesign 15 (USEFUL, [spec.24.review-single-source.1]).
**Counted index.** CODE-7 migration 0184 replaces the 0102 index with `idx_sessions_counted_by_tenant (tenant_id, user_id)` over the 5-state predicate; the tenant and per-user counts are index-only.
**OD-S5.** Decisions 25 and 26 stand; the fence-only sub-question is closed ([f2.human-decisions.OD-S5]).
**OD-S6.** Decision 28: `ReadPrimary` takes a deadline from `--session-primary-read-timeout` (default 5 s); an expired deadline is a primary error at every site; pinned at tiers 1 and 8.
**OD-S7.** Decision 29: the playground hard duration cap counts active time; SPEC-5t states it in §27.2, §27.6, and §10.2; no behavior change, and CODE-6 items 10 and 11 and DOCS-1b reword the flag help, the chart, and the docs mirrors.
**OD-S8.** Decision 16: the adapter heartbeats each registry entry from `session_start` to the session's `session_end` or the entry's removal, independent of `Attach`, and `writeSessionEnd` stops and joins the monitor before its write; `OpenStream`, `busy_unobservable:`, `lastAck`, and `startHeartbeat` are deleted; a missed ack ends the current and every later `Attach` for the entry.
**Reclamation.** Decision 30: `type: mcp` and service-mode sessions, and every session on a gateway with no pod binder, keep idle expiry (`max_idle_time`); a session with no attributable `busy` is judged on frames and platform and connector calls alone. No class of session loses idle reclamation.
**Idle is activity-based.** Decision 10 (owner, 2026-10-10): a runtime reporting `busy` counts as activity, and the plan's "a long silent turn is not idle" is superseded by that decision ([spec.17.review-plan-conformance.1]).
**Bare `heartbeat_ack`.** It answers liveness for every heartbeat monitor on the pod and attributes `busy` to no session; `sessionId` stays optional (required only when `busy` is true). A bare-acking runtime (§15.4 samples, §28.5.3 trace step 8) is judged on frames and platform and connector calls alone and sealed by the adapter after a window with neither (SPEC-5f states when the session is idle-suspended); the reference SDKs echo `sessionId` ([spec.17.fix-G1.1], [spec.17.follow-up-fix.1]).
**Not-sealed keeps the seal.** The "next idle firing completes or lifts" sentence is deleted; a seal outliving its attempt under continuing activity holds messages until a later full idle window or `maxSessionAge` ([spec.17.fix-design-G2.1]).
**Elicitation waits.** They are `running`. The runtime's `lenny/request_elicitation` call is a `CH-MCP-PLATFORM` call in flight, which the adapter counts as activity until it returns, so the wait keeps the session from sealing within `maxElicitationWaitSeconds` (SPEC-5ab, SPEC-5p, CODE-6 item 18.1). The adapter serves this channel (`pkg/adapter/platformmcp.go:40-41`, `platformtoolprovider.go:54-75`).
**Elicitation expiry (OD-E1, Decision 33).** A pod-leaving transition deletes the pending elicitations whose `detail.originPod` is the session, and SPEC-5p2 (§9.2 **Elicitation Timeout Semantics:** item 6) is the single home. The delete key is the raiser, never `interactions.session_id` (the resolver), so the §8.2 child hold is untouched. The expiry is a delete rather than an `expired` phase, because the existing `ErrNotFound` paths already return `ELICITATION_NOT_FOUND`, while a non-pending phase returns 409. The sites are CODE-6 item 19.3's. The §29.3 interaction-resolution row carries no clause for it. SPEC-5p2 states the accepted residuals, and the non-spec Edge cases bullet **A resolution the expiry does not catch.** names their code sites. The label OD-E1 replaces the ledger label "OD-R1 (answer to OD-N4)", which collided with the Standing **Owner answers.** OD-R1.
**Playground idle bound.** SPEC-5t keeps the §27.6 **Idle-timeout override.** MUST and its reclamation sentence, and states that the bound idle-suspends the session or expires one that cannot be idle-suspended, except on the SPEC-5f fault paths, where the session stays `running` under `maxSessionAge`; this reverses the round-19 rewording ([spec.19.fix-design-G1.1]). SPEC-5f's **Origin-scoped override:** points at the clock statement.
**Per-team scope.** The §11.1 per-team concurrency row has no limit surface in code (quota.go:161-172); SPEC-6a omitting it is not a bypass. Refuted in r18; do not refile.
**Heartbeat interval.** `--heartbeat-interval-seconds` (default 30, 0 disables) gates the per-entry monitor; with 0, seals rest on frames and platform and connector calls alone. The spec states no interval.
**Held-pod `/resume` race.** On `errResumeHeldPodNotSuspended`, CODE-4 item 8 calls `enterResumePending` with the read row's `State` set to `awaiting_client_action`; that write's guard miss (CODE-4 item 9) is the only 409 source; `resumeHeldPod` keeps its bare sentinel, so `resume_held_pod_test.go` `errors.Is` holds; `errResumeHeldPodReleased` (CODE-6 item 6) is checked first; an over-cap refusal answers 500 ([non-spec.16.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.17.fix-design-G1.1]).
**`enterResumePending` source state.** It guards on the passed row's `State` (plus `!holdsPod` for `suspended`) and on nothing else; a caller that wants another source state passes a row carrying it ([non-spec.17.review-mechanism.1]).
**Interrupt superseded-row test.** `TestInterruptOnSupersededRowWritesNothing_spec_7_2` asserts only the 409, the unchanged row, no status event, and no re-arm, with no `currentState` ([non-spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]).
**Minimal-state record.** Production never writes or reads the §4.4.5 record (`evictionfallback.Writer` has no non-test caller; cmd/ wires no `EvictionStateLookup`); RECORDS-1 7(q) files it ([non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]).
**Tier-8 held-read outage test.** `TestHeldReadOutageDoesNotStallDelivery_spec_7_2` runs on `newStreamChaosGateway` (which needs an options argument for `Options.Messaging`) with Redis behind `blackholeProxy`; go-redis defaults (3 s ReadTimeout, retries) end a blackholed read in about 12 s, inside the 60 s deliver context ([non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.5.review-reliability.1]).
**MintProto generation test.** Extend `TestMintProto_spec_4_9_1347` to read the lease back through `GetByID`; `LeaseStore.Put` takes the lease by value, so a post-Put assignment fails it ([non-spec.3.fix-design-G2.1]).
**`BindingGeneration` seam test.** Extend `TestCoordinationBindingsBoundAndConnAlive` in cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams_test.go; the Sweeper tests use fakes ([non-spec.5.fix-design-G3.1]).
**Inbox-unavailable parity test.** `TestSendMessageInboxUnavailableParity_spec_15_4` is retargeted to a `resume_pending` seed, because a binderless REST server treats every `suspended` row as held-pod ([non-spec.4.review-test-coverage.1]).
**`bufferAfterResumedSendFailure`.** Named in the read-authority site list; its re-read uses `ReadPrimary`; pinned by extending `TestResumeAndDeliverSendFailureAfterReportUsesFreshState_spec_7_2` with a `laggingGetStore` stale-`running`, stored-`cancelled` case ([non-spec-recheck.1.fix-design-G1.1]).
**CRD comment mirrors.** `RuntimeLimits.MaxSessionAgeSeconds` and the SandboxTemplate field render into charts/lenny/crds through `make generate` and are copied to pkg/embedded/crds; CODE-1's comment rewrite names them ([non-spec-recheck.1.review-fresh.1]).
**Seal RPC carriers.** `SealSession` and `UnsealSession` need no §11.3 row, no §4.7.1 admission or bind-attempt row, no §28 naming-table row, no separate fence statement, no hold-allowlist entry, and no adapter auth-list entry; `fenceReadersExempt` gains both and `operationalFenceSites` goes 12 to 14 in the CODE-6 test list ([spec.12.review-edit-sites.1], [non-spec.11.review-edit-sites.1]).
**Seal citation.** The seal lives in §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract); §4.7 is "Runtime Adapter"; the `_spec_4_7` test-name suffixes stay ([non-spec.11.fix-design-G2.1]).
**Seal refusal stream end.** A `FAILED_PRECONDITION` `session_sealed` end of `Attach` is "any other status, discarded without a report" under the 0091 rule; the §28.5.1 **Timing.** and §28.8 rows owe no edit ([spec.12.review-fresh.1]).
**Fence and the seal.** `CoordinatorFence`, gap or not, resets no per-session adapter state, so the seal survives it; the pod rejects any stale-generation RPC, so a stale `UnsealSession` is refused once the adopter fences ([spec.13.review-mechanism.1], [spec.13.review-reliability.1]).
**Stale release safety.** Only the idle write needs the generation conjunct: an adopter's fence fails a stale replica's interrupt-release checkpoint, and the release row guard loses to any adopter write; `PodExecutor.Release` makes no generation-checked call ([non-spec.19.review-security.1], [non-spec.11.fix-design-G1.1]).
**Lease over-revoke.** SPEC-1g can revoke a zero-recorded lease assigned mid-session after a restore; that is availability, never a leak ([spec.13.review-security.1]).
**Lease persistence.** Credential leases persist as a JSON blob, so the generation needs no migration; only gateway-side writers record it; the token-service `RotateCredentials` RPC has no gateway caller, so no generation test is owed for it ([non-spec.13.review-security.1], [non-spec-recheck.1.review-test-coverage.1]).
**Renewal liveness.** The shipped renewal never checked session liveness; SPEC-1g3's lease-held check is the first stop condition, not a regression ([non-spec.5.review-security.1]).
**Held pod in the shipped watchdog.** The shipped watchdog never releases a held pod; `MaxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` is read only for defaulting ([non-spec.16.review-performance.1]).
**Watchdog scope.** The watchdog is not leader-gated: every replica runs `Tick` (5 s) over every tenant, `sweepMaxAge` and `sweepExpiryWarning` list every row, and `sweepMaxAge` expires an over-cap `suspended` row ([non-spec.16.review-performance.1], [non-spec.19.review-mechanism.1]).
**Idle clock today.** `idleClockRunningStates` includes `awaiting_client_action` and `input_required`; CODE-6 narrows it to `running`; `sweepAwaitingClientAction` reclaims abandoned waits; `sweepIdle` anchors on `UpdatedAt` when `LastAgentActivityAt` is zero ([non-spec.16.review-fresh.1], [non-spec.8.review-mechanism.1]).
**Client-message stamp.** The tree stamps no client message (only agent events, the `await_children` poll, and llmproxy usage call `Stamp`); RECORDS-1 7(r) files it; SPEC-5e counts a client message once the adapter writes it; `Stamper.persist` is async, at most one write per second, 5 s timeout ([non-spec.19.review-mechanism.1], [mechanisms.reconcile.1]).
**§6.2 guarantee clauses.** SPEC-5e keeps the agent-work, `lenny/await_children`, and proxy-mode clauses, reworded to "idle-suspended". Each event stamps the gateway clock, a proxy-mode stream once per relayed chunk (CODE-6 item 18.1, row G4), and SPEC-5f starts no attempt until that clock runs a full window, so idle suspension requires both clocks idle (Decision 16, [mechanism.idle-decision-whole.1]).
**SPEC-5y scope.** SPEC-5y edits §29.4 step 9 only; step 10's "of `maxSessionAge` or of the client idle clock" stays true under Decision 30 ([spec.23.fix-design-G2.1]).
**Edge-case pointers.** Every Edge-cases entry is a pointer, including **Pre-claim failure.** and **`input_required` with an absent client.** ([spec.23.fix-design-G1.1]).
**Per-message and idle costs.** Two Redis peeks and one primary read per path-2 message; idle-suspension checkpoints at most about 11/s at Tier 3 (about 1.1 GB/s); idle claim churn about 11/s; `Checkpointer.Sweep` is serial with no limiter; `lockFlight` is a plain mutex that never joins a capture ([non-spec.8.review-performance.1], [non-spec.13.review-performance.1]).
**Orphan-GC protection.** `PodHasActiveSession` counts non-terminal rows naming the pod, so held-pod `suspended`, `resume_pending`, and `resuming` rows protect their pod; `podclaim.DeleteClaim` deletes by name, and deleting a `claimed` pod's claim drains it ([non-spec.13.review-kubernetes.1]).
**Session-row writes.** Every writer goes through `Store.Update` (the only `UPDATE sessions` is pgstore's), `AdvanceLastSeq` included; `Update` has no retry, floors `coordination_generation`, and scopes by tenant, so `ReadPrimary` adds no cross-tenant path ([non-spec.13.review-performance.1], [non-spec.9.review-reliability.1]).
**Mirror release bound.** The Postgres lease fallback `Get` filters `expires_at`, so the per-sweep `releaseMirror` stops once the 60 s lease lapses, about 4 writes per suspension ([non-spec.13.review-performance.1]).
**Erasure order.** Rank 105 sorts before `sessions` (170); `ValidateOrder` checks FK edges only; tenant deletion Phase 4 (`tenantErasers`) is separate and filed as 7(p); `sessions` carries FORCE RLS, so the leader's per-tenant `ListPodlessSuspended` fits, and the orphan reconciler is the precedent for a leader-only cross-tenant scan ([non-spec.16.review-security.1], [spec.12.review-security.1]).
**Adapter wiring.** Every production main assigns `Runtime` before `NewGRPCServer`; `SDKWarmInProcessRuntime` embeds `InProcessRuntime`; the tap list covers `SocketRuntimeProcess`, `SubprocessExecutor`, `InProcessRuntime`, and `MCPRuntime` ([non-spec.16.review-mechanism.1]).
**Message write path.** Every adapter `message` write goes through `writeSessionEnvelope`; heartbeat, `session_start`, and `session_end` use `WriteEnvelope`; the gateway never calls the adapter `SendMessage` RPC ([non-spec.13.review-mechanism.1]).
**Adapter `Resume` caller.** Only `Binder.Resume` through `resumeOnPod`'s checkpoint branch calls it, and it always claims a fresh entry; the "coordinator-handoff resume" comment at pkg/adapter/resume.go has no caller ([non-spec.19.review-reliability.1]).
**Interrupt tool result.** `postByID` keeps the `{"sessionId"}` input schema; the only parsing consumer is the Go SDK `InterruptSession`; the playground ignores the result; §15.2 defines no output schema ([non-spec.19.review-client-surface.1]).
**Cap counts.** A creation refusal is already `429 QUOTA_EXCEEDED` with `limit`, `active`, and (for user, runtime, global) `scope`; every `CountActiveSessions*` runs on the primary pool ([spec.18.review-client-surface.1], [spec.17.review-performance.1]).
**REST failover baseline.** REST `requireTenantNotSuspended` already reads the primary, so a REST failover regression needs more than a primary read; shipped MCP send made no primary call before `Send` ([non-spec.5.review-performance.1]).
**No pod binder.** A gateway with `--agent-namespace` unset wires no pod binder and, under Decision 30, keeps idle expiry; embedded `lenny up` sets the namespace ([non-spec.13.review-docs-alignment.1]).
**Leader fallback.** Out of cluster `AlwaysLeader` runs every leader job un-elected, and an elector build failure runs them on every replica; the guarded `enterResumePending` write tolerates both; the spec is silent and the gap predates 0092 ([spec-recheck.1.review-kubernetes.1], [non-spec.8.review-kubernetes.1]).
**Step-3a then driver drain.** After step 3a's `enterResumePending`, a same-replica driver can release through `releaseEarlierBinding` with `DispositionFailed` first; step 4 then skips on pointer identity; a drain instead of a recycle is accepted ([non-spec.19.review-mechanism.1]).
**Gates that see nothing here.** No gate parses the §4.7.1 RPC table or ties error-catalog.md to errorclassify; the citation gates resolve line citations only, not bold labels; `coordinator_hold_allowlist_test.go` checks only existing entries; `TestCheckpointManifestColumnSetMatchesMigration0178` is one-way; `lint-migrations` fails a migration number with no test under tests/tier2_component/migrations/ ([non-spec.9.review-applicability.1], [spec.17.review-applicability.1], [non-spec.16.review-applicability.1]).
**Why CODE-4 items exist.** `Binder.Resume` mints no credentials (item 20); session-wide releases revoke a later attempt's leases (items 5.3, 20.4, 24); `sweepResuming`'s nil mutator re-anchors the window (item 15); `Send` returns a ctx error after consumption (item 6); an off-holder `deliverBuffered` fails in `streamFor` (item 8); the funnel's lock gap lets `releaseExecutor` tear down a replacement (item 22); renewal re-mints released leases (item 23) ([restructure.non-spec-to-constraints]).
**`busy` scope.** OD-I1 resolved to B on the owner's answer relayed by the launching session: SPEC-5ac's MUST covers any work for the session; the §15.4.6 **heartbeat busy** row and `heartbeat_busy` check verify `busy: true` while a message is outstanding and never fail a `busy: true` with none; the SDKs report a pending message or a busy hold (CODE-6 item 18.4), and §15.7 **Busy reporting.** states it (SPEC-5ac item 8), which closes the SDK gap the [spec.21] OPEN recorded.
**Connector calls.** OD-I3 resolved to B under the owner's "lack of any activity, period" (summary Decision 30): `connectorToolProvider.Call` counts through `beginToolCall` exactly as `platformToolProvider.Call` does, and both already resolve through `callingSession()` (pkg/adapter/connectortoolprovider.go:46-52, pkg/adapter/platformtoolprovider.go:55-61), so B needs no new session-attribution rule ([f7.apply-OD-I3]). OD-I2 and OD-I4 stay resolved at A ([f7.apply-OD-I2], [f7.apply-OD-I4]).
**SPEC-5ac item 2 scope.** It keeps only the runtime `busy` MUST, the attribution pointer (ending at "**Session seal.** states."), and the liveness sentence; its citers use it only for the MUST, the field table, or the wording ([spec.24.fix-G1.1], [spec.24.fix-design-G1.1]).
**No other `busy` site in spec/.** The only spec hits are §7.2 path 5's "runtime busy" (inbox sense) and a busybox runbook line; the §15.4 samples and the §28.5.3 trace step 8 bare-ack only with no message in hand ([spec.24.review-edit-sites.1]).
**Platform calls on a concurrent pod.** The §28.5.3 `CH-MCP-PLATFORM` **Exclusivity.** bullet refuses a call unless exactly one session resolves, so on a concurrent pod no platform or connector call counts as activity (the §28.5.3 `CH-MCP-CONNECTOR` **Exclusivity.** bullet resolves a connector call the same way, and §28.6 restates it); SPEC-5ab's platform bullet cites the `CH-MCP-PLATFORM` card and its connector bullet the `CH-MCP-CONNECTOR` card ([spec.24.review-mechanism.1], [spec.24.review-edit-sites.1], [spec.25.review-single-source.1]).
**`request_input` return.** `lenny/request_input` is a `CH-MCP-PLATFORM` tool; while in flight the session is `input_required` (clock not evaluated); on return a stale clock fires an attempt at once and the adapter answers not sealed, so the clock repairs itself ([spec.24.review-mechanism.1]).
**OD-S answers in Decisions.** The summary's note on OD-S6 to OD-S10 and the reclamation answer is deleted; the answers live in Decision 16 (OD-S8), Decision 27 (OD-S1, OD-S9, OD-S10), Decision 28 (OD-S6), Decision 29 (OD-S7), and Decision 30 (reclamation) ([f7.cleanup]).
**§29.3 step 1 DLQ hold scope.** The staged "holds the message in the session's DLQ ... and forwards nothing" is scoped by the existing "When the replica serving the call is not the session's coordinating replica" sentence, so it agrees with SPEC-5i **Synchronous delivery.** ([spec.25.review-single-source.1]).
**SPEC-3j traversal reason.** "Its view of a descendant held by another replica is not evidence" is carried over from the existing §29.3 row and stated nowhere else in spec/ ([spec.25.review-single-source.1]).
**Round-25 single-source result.** The connector-call and `busy`-scope delta adds no second stating site: SPEC-5ac item 4 is a clause citing **Outbound: `heartbeat_ack`**, and item 8 (§15.7 **Busy reporting.**) states the SDK's own rule (handler window plus busy holds), citing the runtime MUST; the [spec.24.review-single-source.1] not-filed inventory still holds (USEFUL, [spec.25.review-single-source.1]).
**SDK busy window (Decision 31).** SPEC-5ac item 8 (§15.7 **Busy reporting.**): the pending window runs from reading a `message` until its handler has returned and the SDK has written any response the handler produced; work after the handler returns, first the rest of a streaming, non-final turn, is reported only through a busy hold (Go `HoldBusy(ctx)` through the OnCreate context, Python/TS hold); CODE-6 item 18.4 decrements after the response write ([spec.26.fix-design-G1.1], [spec.27.fix-design-G1.1]).
**SDK busy carriers.** CODE-6 item 18.4 carries the hold scope at the hold-API sub-bullet (a pointer to item 8), the doc-comment sub-bullet, and the docs deliverable clause, and the summary watch-out beginning "The SDK pending-message count rises" restates item 8; a change to item 8 re-points all four; none is a single-source finding ([spec.26.fix-G1.1], [spec.29.review-single-source.1], [non-spec.20.review-single-source.1]).
**Busy-window test.** `TestHeartbeatAckBusyWhileHandlerRuns_spec_28_5_3` asserts `busy` across a blocked `response` write after the handler returns (the Go SDK acks inline on the frame loop through `frameWriter.mu`, which `writeFor` also holds); tier-3 `TestRuntimeSDKHeartbeatBusy_spec_28_5_3` is the cross-SDK pin, so no per-language tier-1 case is owed ([non-spec.20.fix-G1.1], [non-spec.31.review-test-coverage.1]).
**echo-concurrent heartbeat.** CODE-6 item 18.4 routes a heartbeat naming a held session to that session's serial echocore worker, so the ack follows any queued response, and drops the `heartbeat_ack` skip in `slotWriter.stamp`; an unaddressed heartbeat or one naming an unheld session keeps the front-loop bare ack; `TestHeartbeatNamingHeldSessionAckedByItsWorker_spec_28_5_3` pins it ([non-spec.20.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.21.fix-design-G2.1]).
**Serial reference runtimes.** echo (echocore), streaming-echo, delegation-echo, elicitation-echo, the binary-minimal scaffold, the recursive-delegation tutorial runtimes, and the §15.4.4 pseudocode loops handle `message` synchronously, so their bare ack follows the response and passes the `heartbeat_busy` row unchanged; only echo-concurrent needed an edit; every round from 20 to 46 re-confirmed this, so do not re-derive it.
**Compliance check level.** `heartbeat_busy` is a Basic entry in probe.go `checkLevel`; CODE-6 item 18.5 points at the §15.4.6 **heartbeat busy** row (SPEC-5ac item 5) rather than restating it; `TestHeartbeatBusyIsABasicCategory_spec_15_4_6` asserts `failuresAtOrBelow` returns `[heartbeat_busy]`; adding it breaks no probe or compliance count test (counts derive from `basicCases()`) ([non-spec.21.fix-design-G2.1], [non-spec.29.review-edit-sites.1]).
**Hub reader start.** CODE-6 item 18.2: each transport starts `Serve` where it creates the hub; `SubprocessExecutor` serves eagerly only when `TapOutput` has recorded a tap (only the adapter taps, and the adapter's `RuntimeProcess` has no `Send`); untapped executors keep the lazy reader at first `Output`; item 18.2 names the four stale comments (the hub field comments and the `Output` doc comments); the tap tests use "no `Output` call ever made" as the precondition ([non-spec.21.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.20.follow-up-fix.1]).
**Eager reader safety.** `writeAwaitedSessionStart` subscribes before writing `session_start`, the gateway's first `AttachRequest` carries no envelope, `Hub.Serve` is `sync.Once`, `heartbeatMonitor.ack()` is non-blocking, and no adapter path holds `s.mu` across `WriteEnvelope`, so the eager reader drops no frame the gateway reads and the tap cannot deadlock ([non-spec.21.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.29.review-reliability.1], [non-spec.35.review-fresh.1]).
**Activity inventory.** Redesign 1 of 2026-10-11: CODE-6 item 18.1 holds a closed table, with adapter rows A1 to A7 setting `lastActivity` and `toolCalls` and gateway rows G1 to G9 advancing `last_agent_activity_at` (the per-chunk proxy stamp is row G4, `StampAt` row G9); a write of either clock outside the table is a defect; SPEC-5ab's adapter list is closed and includes `llm_request_completed`; CODE-6 items 4 and 5, Decision 16, the summary watch-outs, and DOCS-1b point at the table ([redesign1.apply.2]).
**CH-RUNTIMEOPS activity.** `llm_request_completed` is the only session-scoped runtime-to-adapter CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame; `observeRuntimeOpsSession` is installed by `WireDirectModeUsage` (pkg/adapter/usage.go, the one place `Server` meets `RuntimeOps` before `Run`) through `RuntimeOps.SetActivityObserver`, and stamps on every completion, zero tokens included; its bullet carries its own §28.5.3 (CH-RUNTIMEOPS) citation ([non-spec.37.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.37.follow-up-fix.1]).
**Non-activity frames.** `heartbeat_ack` is not in `sessionScopedFrameTypes`, so only its attributed `busy: true` branch stamps; `checkpoint_ready`, `interrupt_acknowledged`, `credentials_acknowledged`, `lifecycle_support`, `llm_request_started`, `session_started`, and unaddressed frames on a multi-slot pod are not activity, so a periodic Full-level checkpoint does not keep a session from sealing; SPEC-5ab's read list mirrors `sessionScopedFrameTypes` and harmlessly names `message` and `tool_result` ([spec.26.review-fresh.1], [non-spec.35.review-mechanism.1], [spec.30.review-applicability.1]).
**Channels and activity.** The intra-pod channels are CH-MSGSOCK, CH-RUNTIMEOPS, CH-MCP-PLATFORM, and CH-MCP-CONNECTOR; CH-LLMPROXY is pod egress and feeds the gateway clock; CH-OBJSTORE falls under SPEC-5ab's "other channels ... not adapter activity" ([spec.30.review-edit-sites.1], [spec.30.review-single-source.1]).
**Adapter clock.** CODE-6 item 18.1 **Clock.**: a `Server.clock func() time.Time` field plus a nil-safe `now()` method falling back to `time.Now()`, as `SessionUsageMeter` does with its names swapped (field `now`, method `clock()`); it covers `adapter.New` and struct-literal servers; the summary 0084 row names `clock` ([non-spec.25.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.26.review-citations.1]).
**Proxy stamp wiring.** `TestLLMProxyServerWiresActivityStamper_spec_6_2` resolves `POST /llm-proxy/v1/messages` through `srv.Handler.(*http.ServeMux).Handler(req)` and asserts the handler's `Activity`; `newLLMProxyServer` is the only production constructor; `sessionidle.Stamper.Stamp` is nil-receiver safe; the wiring matters because a runtime with no attributed `busy` would be sealed mid-stream without the gateway per-chunk stamp ([non-spec.35.fix-design-G1.1]).
**Not-sealed clock write.** SPEC-5f's not-sealed branch advances `last_agent_activity_at` to the adapter's last activity only when that is later (forward-only, matching `Stamper.persist` and row G9 `StampAt`); the refusal branch's "current time" set is deliberate ([spec.41.fix-design-G1.1]).
**SuspendIdle retry gate.** A failed idle checkpoint records the shared 60 s retry time, the `session.suspension_checkpoint_failed` event, and `lenny_session_suspension_checkpoint_failed_total`, whose §16.1 and metrics.md wording holds for both releases; the CODE-6 tier-1 case asserts no seal at +59 s and a seal at +60 s ([non-spec.25.fix-design-G3.1], [non-spec.31.review-docs-alignment.1]).
**Read-failure invariant scope.** D5 invariant I2 covers session-row reads only; REST `requireTenantNotSuspended` and `requireInjectionSupported` read the primary in `loadMessageTarget` before the lock and answer 500 on error; MCP send reads no tenant row before CODE-7; the edge case **Message during a §12.3 primary failover.** is a pointer to D5 and CODE-7 item 4 ([non-spec.20.fix-design-G3.1], [non-spec.21.review-mechanism.1]).
**Seal-refused re-read answer.** A seal-refused stale-row batch answers `inbox_unavailable` through CODE-6 item 7's held branch, never through `admitResume` (CODE-7 item 4 gates only `ActionResumeAndDeliver`); `TestStaleRowDeliveryRefusedBySeal_spec_6_2` pins it ([non-spec.20.fix-design-G3.1], [non-spec.21.review-single-source.1]).
**admitResume errors.** A non-refusal count error answers REST 500 `INTERNAL_ERROR` and an MCP Go error (not `mcp.NewToolError`) with nothing held, and `ResumeBacklog` returns false, the opposite of its `HeldLen`-error true; the CODE-7 tier-1 message-path and `ResumeBacklog` bullets pin each ([non-spec.26.fix-design-G2.1]).
**Resume-held signatures.** `resumeHeldAndDrain(ctx, row)` and `resumeHeldPod(ctx, row)` take the caller's row (CODE-4 item 8); the row supplies only the `Update` key and the `admitResume` input, and the `suspended`/`holdsPod(*r)` guard and the CODE-1 item 5 age-cap check stay on the locked row; pinned by `TestResumeSuspendedBranchesOnCallerRow_spec_7_2` ([non-spec.29.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.31.review-test-coverage.1]).
**Leader pass paging.** `ListPodlessSuspended(ctx, tenantID, afterID, limit)` orders by `id` with `id > afterID`, and pgstore omits the bound when `afterID` is empty because `sessions.id` is UUID; PageSize 500 and a plain `errgroup.Group` with `SetLimit(32)` (not `WithContext`); a page list error ends that tenant's pass for the tick; the tier-2 `TestListPodlessSuspendedUsesPartialIndex` (EXPLAIN with no Sort node, empty-cursor first page) is the only discriminating test; under default `durableInbox: false` each row costs one ZCARD ([non-spec.25.fix-design-G2.1], [non-spec.26.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.25.review-performance.1]).
**Tier-5 drain test.** `TestStreamFailureConcurrentPoolDrainsAtThreshold_spec_5_2` counts reported sessions by a `retryCount` rise and adds `RetryCount` to `sessiondriver.Session`, because the driver restores the session onto the pool's second warm pod; the tier-4, 7a, and 8 stream-failure fixtures run no watchdog, and the tier-4 interactive cases run binderless, so none needs a disposition ([non-spec.27.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.29.review-test-coverage.1]).
**Heartbeat-ack relay tests.** CODE-6 deletes `TestAttachRelaysAProtocolLevelFrame_spec_28_5_3` and tier-3 `TestProtocolFrameIsRelayedOnAPodHoldingTwoSlots_spec_28_5_3` with their spec-map entries, because the rewritten `TestAttachConsumesHeartbeatAck_spec_15_4_1_1453` asserts no relay; the slotframe demux cases stay valid ([non-spec.22.fix-design-G1.1]).
**Fence-count statements.** tests/tier0_static/adapter_proto_generation_scope_test.go states the operational fence count four times (the constant, two "twelve" comments, and the self-test's `want 12` literal); all four go to 14, and the literal stays a literal ([non-spec.21.fix-design-G3.1]).
**Change-graph keys.** Each new fixture dir (always-busy, bare-ack-while-handling) gets its own tests/change-graph.json glob key; a later lens found a baseline prefix may already cover them, so the key is harmless; new files directly in a baselined dir such as pkg/adapter/ need none ([non-spec.23.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.25.review-fresh.1], [non-spec.25.review-edit-sites.1]).
**Manifest tier 11.** Any step that regenerates pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml (S15, S16) declares tier 11, because `TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender_spec_17_4` byte-compares it ([non-spec.20.fix-G5.1]).
**DOCS-1b runtime-author pages.** The busy bullet lists lifecycle.md **Health Checks**, the concepts.md Basic **Heartbeat:** bullet, build-a-runtime.md **Heartbeats** and its calculator case, the local-development.md troubleshooting row, and sdk-examples/index.md item 4, points every missed-ack outcome at the S8 adapter-contract.md `heartbeat` sentence, and names the frames that are not activity (`heartbeat`, `session_end`, `heartbeat_ack`) ([non-spec.20.fix-design-G4.1], [non-spec.25.fix-design-G4.1]).
**Binderless local modes.** `make run` and compose pass no `--agent-namespace`, so no pod binder is built and idle sessions expire with `max_idle_time`; CODE-6 item 5's "(§17.4 Source Mode runs an agent container)" is true of spec/17 but not of the tree; DOCS-1b stages a local-development.md line and CODE-6 item 5 names it (OD-R3 of [redesign1.apply.2], kept) ([non-spec.26.review-docs-alignment.1], [f9.human-decisions.OD-R3]).
**RECORDS-1 item 8 deferral.** `Session inbox redelivery` defers to R19; item 8 stages the seed-claim-register.py override-loop extension that copies an override's `deferral_id`, because the loop otherwise applies only `surface`, `note`, and `status` and `inbox|redeliver` maps to R20; an override must carry `note` ([non-spec.33.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.34.review-applicability.1]).
**Plan filings.** 0092's plan obligations sit in §10.2 and in the R19/R20 "Re-derived against proposal 0092" paragraphs; RECORDS-1 7(b), 7(f), 7(h), 7(i), 7(j), and 7(n) file the latter; 0091 RECORDS-1 items 4, 10, 11, 16, and 17 are F-7.3.29, F-10.1.25, F-7.3.30, F-10.1.26, and F-7.2.27; F-7.2.26 names no 0092 owner ([non-spec.25.review-plan-conformance.1], [non-spec.35.review-plan-conformance.1], [non-spec.42.review-plan-conformance.1]).
**Credential rejection (Decision 34).** SPEC-1g states only the refusal predicate (a refusal that recurs on every attempt); SPEC-1b item 3 maps it to `credential_rejected`; SPEC-1h adds it to the default `nonRetryableFailures`, which only documents §7.3 step 2 (a reason on neither list is non-retryable); a client list naming it makes it retryable, bounded by `maxRetries`; it has no client enum or schema parallel ([spec.30.fix-design-G2.1], [spec.31.review-single-source.1], [spec.35.review-reliability.1]).
**`isCredentialRejection`.** A positive list; gRPC `FailedPrecondition` and `Unauthenticated` reach the driver only from a transport or interceptor rejection, which SPEC-1g covers; an adapter `AssignCredentials` RPC error is not a `CredentialAssignmentError` and stays `runtime_crash`; the refusal chain survives the slot and rebuild paths ([non-spec.39.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.38.review-mechanism.1]).
**Pre-claim deliveryMode check.** SPEC-1g's "session-start `deliveryMode` check" is the §4.9 admission check at the end of the SPIFFE-binding note (spec/04:1628); the code evaluates the pool match (`checkCredentialDeliveryIsolation`), so pre-claim placement is workable and is not a security regression; a plain grep of §4.9 for the phrase finds nothing ([spec.31.review-fresh.1], [spec.35.review-mechanism.1], [spec.37.review-security.1]).
**Entry paths home.** §7.3 **Entry paths:** (staged by SPEC-1h) is the one complete home of `awaiting_client_action` entry routes, reading "in these ways:" with no count: (a) retry exhaustion, (b) `maxResumeWindowSeconds`, (c) a non-retryable `resuming` failure, (d) BUDGET_STATE_UNRECOVERABLE through §11.2 **Irrecoverable trees:**; SPEC-3l carries no route clause ([spec.30.fix-design-G2.1], [spec.31.fix-design-G1.1]).
**`awaiting_client_action` may hold a pod.** Path (d)'s marker writes the state from any non-terminal state and leaves `PodAssignment` set, so SPEC-2a, the SPEC-5e row, and summary items 4, 5, and 10 carry no podless claim; name the store-write resume sources as "`awaiting_client_action` and podless `suspended`" ([spec.32.fix-design-G1.1], [spec.32.follow-up-fix.1]).
**Elicitation expiry text.** SPEC-5p2 is one unnumbered edit inserting §9.2 **Elicitation Timeout Semantics:** item 6; its child-elicitation clause cites §8.2 **Replay on resume:** (trigger-neutral), and the re-raise carries no citation; it expires only `lenny/request_elicitation` elicitations (connector URL-mode ones stay pending); one whose expiry fails stays pending, with no reclaimer beyond a live call's `maxElicitationWait`; the §29.3 interaction-resolution row carries no clause ([spec.31.fix-design-G2.1], [spec.37.review-security.1], [redesign1.apply.3]).
**Elicitation keys.** `request_elicitation` writes `detail.originPod = row.ID` (the raiser), `UserID = row.UserID`, and `SessionID` = the resolver; a child's call polls `Interactions.Get`, so a resolution recorded on any replica reaches it with no forward; an expired elicitation answers the existing §15.1 `404 ELICITATION_NOT_FOUND` ("does not match any pending elicitation"); `INTERACTION_ALREADY_RESOLVED` appears in no spec file ([spec.30.fix-design-G3.1], [non-spec.39.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.20.fix-design-G5.1]).
**Resume-attempts counter scope.** SPEC-5q: each `resuming` incarnation whose entry write the driver observes take effect counts once, `success` on an observed landed commit (Landed, Relinquished, Above `G`) and `failure` on a reported failure or an observed miss (including `ErrNotFound`); the hook store error, a shutdown cut-off, a 3.7 report after the server context ends, and the binderless commit are uncounted; the CODE-4 item 25 table and its **Comments.** sub-bullet implement it, and DOCS-1b rewrites pod-kill-during-session.md:74; no alert or SLO reads the counter ([mechanism.resume-attempts-counter.1], [non-spec.38.fix-design-G1.1], [spec.46.review-mechanism.1]).
**Takeover delivery (OD-H1, Decision 36).** SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.**: adoption of a `running` session together with its pod is a delivery point; the committing replica delivers only while it is still coordinator, otherwise the adopter does, through CODE-5 item 2c `HeldDeliverer` (lands unset at S12) and CODE-4 item 26 `DeliverHeldAfterTakeover` (S14); adoption emits no `status_change`, `session.resumed`, or `children_reattached`; Decision 36 rests on the §7.2 durable-inbox Crash recovery row ([redesign1.apply.4], [f9.human-decisions.OD-H1], [non-spec.43.review-single-source.1]).
**Tail owner (OD-H2, Decision 35).** The resumer runs the bus, audit, cleanup, and tree-recovery rows on the **Landed**, **Relinquished**, and **Above `G`** columns; Decision 35 is a column citation rather than an effect list; **Context ended** runs none, which is accepted because §10.4 handoff synthesis covers it; the tail test's **Discriminating assertions.** keep only the Landed `partial_workspace` ordering and the Not-landed chunk-object check ([non-spec.42.fix-design-G1.1], [redesign1.apply.5], [spec.39.review-fresh.1]).
**Tail exactly-once.** Every client-visible tail effect (`status_change(running)`, `session.resumed`, `children_reattached`, held delivery) fires at most once per landed commit on the binder path; the D3 lock orders a same-replica adopter's item 26 delivery after the resumer's tail; nothing orders held output against `session.resumed` across replicas, and the spec does not require it ([non-spec.40.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.42.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.45.review-single-source.1]).
**No-message held delivery.** SPEC-5i defers to the idle suspension and **Backlog resume.** "as each states", or to the session's expiry; an interrupt suspension's backlog predicate ignores entries accepted before `SuspendedAt`, so such an entry waits for a client resume or expiry; the quoted phrase "except while the idle suspension does not complete" in older ledger entries is gone, and the behavior it recorded is unchanged ([spec.42.fix-design-G1.1]).
**Cancel citations.** SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.** cites snapshot-close step 3 for the attempt's next write and states the same-replica cancel per step 1; a terminal write on another replica reaches the attempt no later than its next write (Decision 21) ([spec.43.fix-design-G1.1], [spec.46.review-citations.1]).
**Mid-resume terminal writers.** DELETE, parent cascade, admin cancel, and `sweepMaxAge` can land on a `resuming` row; `POST /terminate` never admits `resuming` ([non-spec.42.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.42.review-citations.1]).
**`heartbeat_ack` echo exception.** SPEC-5ac item 9 (§28.5.3 **Message schemas**) and item 10 (the §15.4.3 MessageEnvelope fields row) except `heartbeat_ack` from the Basic `sessionId` echo; every other echo site (spec/28:625, :1007, spec/15:2068, spec/24:253) answers a `message`; DOCS-1b stages the exception on adapter-contract.md **Basic-level runtimes:**, integration-levels.md **Minimal message fields**, and runtime-author-guide/index.md ([spec.39.fix-design-G1.1], [spec.39.follow-up-fix.1], [spec.40.review-edit-sites.1], [redesign1.apply.5]).
**Availability population (Decision 32).** SPEC-6e: the §16.5 availability denominator `CountActiveSessionsGlobal` excludes `suspended`; no docs page states a denominator, and the slo.go "fraction of active sessions" comment stays true ([redesign1.apply.2], [non-spec.26.review-docs-alignment.1], [non-spec.45.review-edit-sites.1]).
**Lease liveness.** §6.1 **Per-session credential lease lifecycle.** ties session end to lease revocation, so SPEC-1g3's lease-record stop drops no stated stop condition, and SPEC-1g3 stays unchanged ([spec.39.review-security.1] CORRECTS [spec.38.review-security.1]).
**Claim deletes.** A gateway `podclaim.DeleteClaim` while the pod projects `claimed` drains it, and only a `reserved` pod returns to idle, so CODE-3's new release paths fail closed; the gateway SA already holds sandboxclaims delete; the resume claim gets its first `bound` patch at claim time, so SPEC-1b correctly cites the **Live binding states** predicate ([non-spec.31.review-kubernetes.1], [spec.37.review-kubernetes.1]).
**Tenant suspension and revoke.** Tenant suspend force-terminates every non-terminal row, podless `suspended` included, and §11.4 full revoke cancels them, so no resume path revives one and resume needs no TENANT_SUSPENDED gate; §12.8 processing restriction gates only creation ([non-spec.31.review-security.1], [spec.30.review-security.1]).
**Idle release disposition.** The idle release takes the pool's session-end disposition (recycle with per-slot cleanup, scrub, and tenant pin on a recycling pool; drain otherwise), which tightens reuse; a restore's claim keeps §5.2 tenant pinning ([non-spec.35.review-security.1], [spec.46.review-security.1], [non-spec.20.review-security.1]).
**PreMessageDelivery scope.** The phase covers inter-session `lenny/send_message` only, so REST messages held without an interceptor run bypass nothing; a held MCP message passes the chain before the hold (CODE-6 item 7) ([non-spec.20.review-security.1], [spec.46.review-security.1]).
**Stamper behavior.** `sessionidle.Stamper.Stamp` is async, coalesced to one write per second per session, persisted under a 5 s timeout, forward-only, nil-safe, and drops errors, so the proxy stamp cannot stall a relay and a refused seal's lost stamp during a failover only makes the holder ask again next tick; `persist` advances `UpdatedAt` on any non-terminal row (pre-existing); client messages do not stamp ([non-spec.35.review-performance.1], [non-spec.37.review-reliability.1], [non-spec.26.review-mechanism.1]).
**Refused-seal cost.** A not-sealed answer costs one `SealSession` RPC and one uncoalesced `StampAt` per idle window per session, using durations only, so clock skew does not matter ([non-spec.35.review-performance.1]).
**Runtime soft delete.** runtimestore `Get` returns soft-deleted rows, so `IdleSuspendable`'s resolve error is transient only ([non-spec.37.review-reliability.1]).
**Heartbeat monitor lifecycle.** Every failed start removes the registry entry, so "start only when `entry.hb` is nil" never strands an entry; monitor write errors occur only when the transport is gone; `writeSessionEnd`'s `stop()` join adds no hang beyond its own write; the heartbeat-miss `Interrupt` lives in the monitor's `onHeartbeatHung`, not in `Attach`; a missed-ack `DeadlineExceeded` end of `Attach` is a reported stream failure; heartbeats default on (30 s interval, 10 s ack timeout) ([non-spec.25.review-reliability.1], [non-spec.31.review-reliability.1], [non-spec.37.review-single-source.1]).
**Sweeper and binder.** A Sweeper runs with no pod binder whenever Redis is wired (redis_quota.go), and only the readopt seam fails closed; `readoptAndFence` publishes a binding that carries `Adapter`, so an adopter's `SuspendIdle` is not skipped; adoption is level-triggered, so a podless row moved to `resume_pending` is adopted on the next sweep ([non-spec.45.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.35.review-reliability.1], [spec.37.review-mechanism.1]).
**Fence generation.** `coordfence.Fencer.Fence` fences at the row's current generation and writes none; §10.1.2 gap detection leaves `last_fenced_generation` unset until the first fence, so the entry increment makes no spurious gap; the partial-manifest `MAX(coordination_generation)` keys on manifest rows ([non-spec.45.review-mechanism.1], [spec.46.review-mechanism.1]).
**DLQ mechanics.** The terminal drain reads and deletes in one MULTI (ZRANGE + DEL), so SPEC-5i's "the drain took them" holds; `MigrateInboxToDLQ` keeps each message's `EnqueuedAt`; the backlog `Idle` clause is unreachable on the coordinator's held-pod evaluation and live at the releaser and the leader, whose inbox read sees only a durable inbox ([spec.37.review-reliability.1], [non-spec.37.review-mechanism.1], [spec.36.review-mechanism.1]).
**Tail facts.** `recoverDelegationTree` is fire-and-forget; `classifyResumeWithAdapter` makes no pod RPC; the shipped tail ran the partial-manifest cleanup before classification, so `ResumePartialWorkspace` never fired on a wired gateway; `ClearInboxOnAcquire` only emits `inbox_cleared`, so deleting `clearInboxOnResume` removes no loss path, and its test dispositions are staged ([non-spec.44.review-fresh.1], [redesign1.apply.4], [non-spec.43.review-fresh.1]).
**Client-surface parallels.** openapi.json response maps are partial (no session route lists 429), so CODE-7 owes no openapi edit; `generated_schemas.go` carries only `retryPolicy.maxSessionAgeSeconds`; Python/TS client MCP expose only create_session and send_message, and only the Go client has `InterruptSession` (sole caller localcli); retry failure lists are free strings; `SuspendedReason` and failure reasons reach no client; `registerClientFacingTools` returns early when `SessionService` is nil ([non-spec.25.review-client-surface.1], [non-spec.37.review-client-surface.1], [spec.38.review-client-surface.1]).
**Flag and chart carriers.** No docs page or chart names a gateway flag (`--max-idle-time-seconds`, the resume flags, `--session-primary-read-timeout`, `--stream-failure-report-timeout`); the chart's `max-idle-time-seconds` is the playground flag; the chart renders caps as `LENNY_*` env vars; `gateway` and `delegation` are open objects in values.schema.json and `playground` is closed ([non-spec.29.review-edit-sites.1], [non-spec.33.review-edit-sites.1], [non-spec.35.review-edit-sites.1]).
**Metric registration.** `lenny_session_pod_released_during_suspension_total` and `lenny_session_suspension_checkpoint_failed_total` exist only in catalog.go, so CODE-6 item 4's increments force collector registration; no alert reads any suspension or resume metric; `InboxDrainFailure` appears in docs only in the generated rules.yaml ([non-spec.33.review-fresh.1], [non-spec.25.review-docs-alignment.1], [non-spec.35.review-docs-alignment.1]).
**Count queries.** Every `CountActiveSessions*` runs on the primary, and the callers are quota.go and `exportSessionAvailabilityRatio`; the 0102 index serves only the tenant count; 0080 excludes empty `pod_assignment`; `requireSessionQuota`'s `active >= limit` is correct for resume because the count excludes the resuming row; the CapRefusal bodies match creation's verbatim, and a 429 is cached for 24 h ([non-spec.31.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.25.review-performance.1], [spec.37.review-client-surface.1]).
**Migrations.** The tree ends at 0181; 0182 (CODE-1), 0183 (CODE-6), 0184 (CODE-7), and 0185 (CODE-6 item 19) land in checklist order ([f9.human-decisions.OD-1], [redesign1.apply.3]).
**Compile-only test breaks.** The `newLLMProxyServer` positional calls in main_test.go, `ClaimSessionForTest` calling `markSessionStartWritten`, the 3-arg `newHeartbeatMonitor` calls, the `resumeHeldPod` call sites in resume_held_pod_test.go, and `TestResolverDefaultsToEffectiveMaxSessionAge_spec_6_2` are covered by the Testing preamble's existing-test rule; do not file them ([non-spec.36.review-test-coverage.1], [non-spec.31.review-reliability.1], [non-spec.25.review-test-coverage.1]).
