### [restructure.tests-as-intents]
DECISION: the owner approved restating every test obligation in non-spec-changes.md `## Testing` as an intent, and the restructure landed on 2026-10-12. Each obligation is one labelled bullet (`[T0.n]` for TEST-0, `[C1.n]` to `[C7.n]` for the tests that land with each code deliverable, and `[T1.n]` for TEST-1) carrying **Intent:** (the behavior the test proves, in one sentence), **Tier:** (the tier numbers of `.claude/rules/test-coverage.md`), **Spec:** (sections cited by heading), and an optional **Discriminates:** clause naming the wrong implementation the test must fail on. The implementor chooses test names, fixtures, helpers, seeding, fakes, assertion mechanics, and file placement; each intent is pinned by at least one test at each named tier, annotated with its `// spec:` sections. Table-driven obligations name the table they iterate (for example every row of the CODE-4 item 25 table) rather than listing rows. Every "Pinned by" citation in the design, the code items, the activity inventory table, RECORDS-1, the summary, and the checklist names intent labels in place of test function names. The terminal writer list that a test body used to hold moved, unchanged, into Design **Terminal transition.** (D7) as the **Terminal writer inventory.** table, and intent C4.49 pins it. BECAUSE about 270 fixture-level test descriptions were re-checked against the tree in every review round, which cost a full pass per round and produced findings about fixtures rather than design defects. ALTERNATIVES: keep fixture-level tests (rejected by the owner for that cost); keep names only for cited tests (rejected because every citation could point at an intent label instead).
WATCHOUT: do not file a finding asking for a test name, a fixture, a helper, a fake configuration, a seeding step, an assertion mechanism, or a test file path. A test-side finding is in scope only when an intent is missing a behavior, failure path, concurrency case, tier, or spec section that the design or a CODE item requires, or when an intent contradicts the design. Add or amend an intent in the same one-bullet form; do not restore fixture-level text. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md `## Testing` preamble.

### [redesign1.apply.9]
FACT: Applied scratchpad/redesign/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway-redesign-1.md (staging defects, lease return, adjacent defects, expansive tests, and failure triage): N1 to N83 with N62a, N65a, N82a, and N82b, SC1 to SC4, S1 to S9 with S2a, and CL1 to CL5. Every anchor matched exactly once, and no edit was skipped. EVIDENCE: git diff of the proposal directory.
DECISION: Redesigned five areas. (1) The 0092-A1 to A20 staging defects: an ambiguous commit miss counts as landed only on its own evidence (the attempt's sandbox and a `RecoveryGeneration` above the step-1 row), `ReleaseUncommitted` reuses `failResume`'s teardown and releases nothing after a store error unless the reclaim answers `RECLAIMED`, the fence runs at `G` through the new `FenceAt`, the post-commit terminal re-check runs on the Relinquished column, `deliverBuffered` gains a **Terminal stop.**, the suspension release and `SuspendIdle` check this replica's terminal claims, and `lenny/request_elicitation` reads the primary. (2) One rule for credential lease return, D6 **Credential lease return.**, with lease expiry as the accepted fallback (Edge cases **Credential leases with no binding releaser.**) and the `AssignProto` and `MintProto` error returns (CODE-4 item 24.7). WATCHOUT: Replaced: the `resumeHeldPod` refusal of an over-cap row (CODE-1 item 5, intent C1.4) and the age filter in `sweepResumePending` (CODE-4 item 1).
  It lost because `sweepMaxAge` does not expire a `suspended` row (SPEC-5g), so the refusal refused every later message to that session, and a filter in one caller left `ReattachNode` unchecked.
  The age check is in `DriveResume` on the step-1 primary row; `resumeHeldPod` admits an over-cap row and `sweepMaxAge` expires it from `running` (Edge cases **Over-cap row entering `running`.**). Do not restore the refusal unless OD-TT1 is answered B.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 5, CODE-4 items 1 and 2; summary OD-TT1
WATCHOUT: Replaced: `ReleaseUncommitted` in `release.go` as the credential-free teardown of a split `Release` and `ReleaseSlot`, releasing on every `commitNotLanded` exit.
  It lost because after a store error the commit may have landed and the pod may have passed to an adopter, so an unconditional release could free another owner's pod, slot, or leases, and the split duplicated `failResume`'s teardown.
  `ReleaseUncommitted` shares `failResume`'s teardown and, with `requireReclaimed`, releases nothing unless the reclaim answers `RECLAIMED`; the leases then wait for a later pod release or expiry. Do not release on any other reply.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5.3; spec-changes.md SPEC-1b, SPEC-1g; Edge cases **Credential leases with no binding releaser.**
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the commit classification that counted a miss after a store error as landed when `R` showed `podHeld` at `G` on the result's sandbox.
  It lost because a later incarnation on a concurrent pool's shared pod, or a terminal row, matches that test without this commit having landed or rules it out wrongly.
  A miss counts as landed only on the commit's own evidence: the result's sandbox and a `RecoveryGeneration` above the step-1 row (`b.recoveryGen`), with `podHeld(R)` or a terminal `R`. Do not drop the `RecoveryGeneration` conjunct.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5.3 classification table; intent C4.14
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the `full_revoke` cancel and drain on `podTerminateFanOut` (a `drainer` field, `TerminateUserSessions` draining, `terminateLocal` cancelling).
  It lost because the fan-out is constructed only where a pod registry is wired and also runs on peers through propagation, while the §11.4 cancel and drain belong right after each write on the writing replica.
  `terminateUserSessions` calls the session server's `AfterRevokeWrite` right after each `cancelled` write through the `admin.Router` field `revoked`; `user_revocation.go` is not touched. Do not move the cancel or drain back onto the fan-out.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 2a, D7 **Seams.**; intents C4.59 to C4.61
WATCHOUT: Replaced: tree recovery that selected nodes by absence from the local pod registry (`nodeNeedsRecovery`) and a `ReattachNode` that returned nil for any non-`resume_pending` node or right after calling `DriveResume`.
  It lost because `podsession.Registry` holds only this replica's bindings, so a peer-bound node read as orphaned (TT-48), and `recovery.Recover` records any nil return as `OutcomeRecovered`.
  Selection is by row state `resume_pending`; every selected node of a level is driven before any wait, and `ReattachNode` polls the primary row until the restore's outcome under its node deadline. Do not reintroduce registry-based selection or an early nil return.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 11; intents C4.20 and T1.36; RECORDS-1 item 6c
WATCHOUT: Replaced: `fenceResumedPod` fencing through `Fence`, which retries at a re-read generation.
  It lost because a takeover racing the commit raised the generation, and a retry at the re-read value stamped the binding with a generation the pod never accepted at `G`.
  `fenceResumedPod` passes `G` to the new `FenceAt`, which relinquishes on a stale rejection; the readopt keeps `Fence`. Do not retry the resume fence at a re-read value.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5 step 4; intent C4.63
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the edge case **Concurrent-pool counter after an ambiguous commit.** and RECORDS-1 items 7(c) and 7(k).
  The edge case lost because `ReleaseUncommitted` no longer releases a slot without a `RECLAIMED` reply; 7(c) and 7(k) lost because BUILD-GAPS already carries F-8.10.18, F-10.1.34, and F-11.3.40, and RECORDS-1 refers to an existing finding by its F-id and files none again.
  The remaining slot and claim departures are **Uncommitted replacement pod disposition.** and **Terminal row with no binding at its generation.** (owner P1). Do not refile the deleted letters; the other letters keep their names so cross-references resolve.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md RECORDS-1 lead and item 7; Edge cases
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the §7.2 snapshot-close bump in `MarkTerminated` only for `cancelled` or `completed` from `resuming`.
  It lost because SPEC-1b treats every terminal write from `resuming` as a snapshot close (0092-A17 in `scratchpad/terminal-transition/distinct-defects.md`), so a `failed` or `expired` write from `resuming` skipped the bump that SPEC-1b requires.
  `MarkTerminated` bumps `CoordinationGeneration` by one for every terminal state written from `resuming` (D7 **Mark.**, intent C4.48). Do not narrow the condition to two states again.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md D7 **Mark.**; intent C4.48

### [spec.52.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: returned no findings in spec round 52 under the end-to-end mechanism lens — BECAUSE the only uncommitted spec-staging delta is still SPEC-5b step 2's retire clause, and it agrees with the stream-failure definition at spec/28_communication-channels.md:256 (a stream the gateway did not end, ending DEADLINE_EXCEEDED or INTERNAL), with spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:532, and with SPEC-5ab's "`session_sealed` is not a stream failure" (FAILED_PRECONDITION is outside the :256 definition) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the non-spec "release unbinds before endConn" gap as a spec contradiction (rejected: a release that evicts the conn is a gateway-ended stream, so :256 does not call it a stream failure).
FACT: the unconfirmed-reclaim lease rule closes end to end: an attempt whose commit try hit a store error keeps leases recorded at its entry generation G; every later restore binds at G' > G and its pod release returns leases at or below G', and a landed-then-overtaken commit's reclaim carries G and is rejected stale by a pod an adopter fenced at G+1, which is why the unconfirmed branch releases nothing. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**, SPEC-1g; non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5 (`ReleaseUncommitted` requireReclaimed, `FenceAt`).
USEFUL [spec.51.review-fresh.1]: its note that the delta is one clause and that spec/ is unchanged since 2026-10-06 let this round spend its budget on whole-flow traces rather than anchor re-verification.

FACT: claimOrphanTimeout defaults to 5 minutes, equal to the 300 s `resuming` watchdog, so SPEC-1b's accepted "restore still running at that age loses its pod" and the watchdog bound coincide — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:241

### [redesign1.apply.11]
FACT: Applied scratchpad/redesign/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway-redesign-1.md (invariant checker and placement, crash-mark window, uncounted slot): SC1, SC2, N0 to N28 with N19a, SM1 to SM5, RL1 to RL3, and CK1 to CK4. Every anchor matched exactly once, and no edit was skipped.
DECISION: Redesigned four areas. The invariant checker now has an explicit tick set (Sweeper `Sweep`, watchdog `Tick`, and from S15 the leader's backlog re-check), waits for started goroutines, stops at a stated horizon, and runs only after X1, X3, X4, and X5 cells, X2 runs, and X2a steps. I1, I3, I4, I5, I6, and I7 were rewritten to what quiescence can assert, and the relaxation register now cites one Edge-cases bullet per row, with new bullets for duplicate held delivery (R20) and a pre-running claim at a `none` terminal write (RECORDS-1 7(x)). Testing **Step placement.** lands each cell in the step that lands the last code it exercises, and one cost statement in the **Expansive lifecycle tests** opening paragraph replaces the per-intent cost text. ### [spec.53.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: Staged SPEC-1c2 replacing the §6.2 **`resume_pending` wall-clock cap:** sentence "If a pod is allocated before the timer fires, the timer is cancelled ..." with "The timer stops when the session leaves `resume_pending`, which a restore does by the resume driver's `resume_pending → resuming` write (§7.3 **Resume driver.**)." — BECAUSE allocation (claim) precedes the conditional entry write in SPEC-1b, so cancelling at allocation leaves a claimed-but-never-entered session unbounded; keying on leaving the state also covers the SPEC-1e terminal collapses — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "stops when the driver's write takes effect" (names one exit only); placing it under SPEC-1e or SPEC-1a (those edit §7.2/§7.3, wrong locator).
FACT: Landed watchdog already bounds by state only: `sweepResumePending` moves a row to `awaiting_client_action` when `now.Sub(row.UpdatedAt)` exceeds the window, with no claim condition — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go (sweepResumePending); so no non-spec change follows.
OPEN: Allocation-keyed descriptions of the `resume_pending → resuming` trigger predate the driver's claim/entry split and were left untouched as a separate finding: spec/07 §7.2 state block ("pod allocated within maxResumeWindowSeconds"), §28.5.1 CH-ATTACH, §29.8, docs/api/internal.md resume_pending bullet, docs/reference/state-machines.md. §7.3 step 3c ("fires before pod is allocated") is a true subset and stays.

### [spec.53.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the §6.2 window finding with one in-place replacement staged as a new SPEC-1c2 (§6.2 **`resume_pending` wall-clock cap:**). The sentence "If a pod is allocated before the timer fires, the timer is cancelled and the session proceeds to `resuming`." becomes "The timer stops when the session leaves `resume_pending`, which a restore does by the resume driver's `resume_pending → resuming` write ([Section 7.3] **Resume driver.**)." BECAUSE the cap paragraph is the only site saying the timer is *cancelled*; the leave-the-state wording also covers the SPEC-1e terminal collapses — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's text "stops when the driver's write takes effect" (omits terminal exits from resume_pending); staging it as SPEC-1e item 7 (SPEC-1e is §7.2, not §6.2).
FACT: code already matches the fixed rule: `sweepResumePending` in pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go writes awaiting_client_action on `now - UpdatedAt > window`, guarded only on the state, and cancels nothing at claim; CODE-4 item 3.2 bounds the attempt by the same window. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:719-745
WATCHOUT: the allocation-keyed *transition* descriptions are a separate claim from the timer-cancel rule, and this design leaves them in place: spec/07:198 "(pod allocated within maxResumeWindowSeconds)", spec/07:199 "(… no pod available)", spec/28 §28.5.1 CH-ATTACH Degradation lines 266-267 (SPEC-1a2 rewrites only the first half), spec/29:1058-1059, docs/api/internal.md:204, and docs/reference/state-machines.md:64. Step 3c (spec/07:415) is a subset statement and stays true, so SPEC-1b item 1's "step 3c bounds the wait" holds.
OPEN: should a later round re-key those transition descriptions on the entry write? They were already in tension with SPEC-1b before this fix. The only exposure is a crash between claim and entry write, where they are loose rather than contradictory.

DECISION: Redesigned one area, the stop rule of the `maxResumeWindowSeconds` cap. The §6.2 **`resume_pending` wall-clock cap:** paragraph (SPEC-1c2) is its single home: the timer starts on entry to `resume_pending` and stops on any exit from it. SPEC-1a step 3c, SPEC-1e items 7 and 8, SPEC-1h (b), SPEC-3n, and DOCS-1a's `internal.md` bullet now name the cap and point at the home, and the SPEC-1b **Resume driver.** lead no longer restates the window. SPEC-1c2 also drops the false "same field that governs `awaiting_client_action` expiry" clause, because §7.3 times that expiry with `maxAwaitingClientActionSeconds`. DOCS-1b gained the `maxResumeWindow` row of session-lifecycle.md, the `session.maxResumeWindowSeconds` row of reference/configuration.md, and the concepts.md `awaiting_client_action` paragraph, each of which keyed the window's end on a pod becoming available — BECAUSE rounds 55 and 56 kept re-filing the window predicate at one carrier after another, and each carrier restated the stop condition in its own words — ALTERNATIVES: restating the stop condition at each carrier in matching words (the round-55 approach).
FACT: Reconciliation beyond the edit list: the summary `## Open decisions for human to make` gained OD-3 for the redesign's open decision 1, with default A applied (edit 10). The summary Decisions and watch-outs needed no change, because Decision 2's `UpdatedAt`-anchored window is the code behavior edit 10 records. The checklist needed no change, because S17 already lands DOCS-1b and names configuration, concepts, and the session lifecycle. Files touched needed no change, because "The DOCS-1a and DOCS-1b pages" covers the added targets. Testing needed no change, because no edit adds a mechanism, intent **[C4.1]** pins the predicate, and no test under `tests/`, `pkg/`, or `cmd/` asserts the replaced docs or spec wording.
FACT: Deleted: the SPEC-1b clause "while the session's `maxResumeWindowSeconds` window is open"; the SPEC-1a step 3c qualifier "while the session is in `resume_pending`"; the SPEC-1h replacement "elapsed before the session left `resume_pending`"; the SPEC-1e item 7 qualifier "within maxResumeWindowSeconds"; the SPEC-3n "the whole sequence" replacement wording; and the stale Standing context text of **SPEC-1a2.** (which discharges the OPEN of [spec.56.fix-G1.1] and [spec.56.fix-design-G1.1]).

The remaining hits are spec-text anchors the implementor replaces (SPEC-1a step 3c, SPEC-1a2, SPEC-1c2, SPEC-1e items 7 and 8, SPEC-1h, SPEC-3n) and the new DOCS-1a sentence that names the cap.

WATCHOUT: Replaced: the `resume_pending` window's end keyed on allocation ("pod allocated within `maxResumeWindowSeconds`", "no pod available") and then restated per carrier ("while the session is in `resume_pending`", "elapsed before the session left `resume_pending`", "while the window is open").

Rejected because it is settled as accepted by the no-forward redesign ([spec.47-era] USEFUL at review-log "Cap exemption / No suspended forward / Leader capacity"). (2) The per-tick coordinator backlog check on held-pod rows. Rejected because idle suspensions hold no pod, so those rows are interrupt suspensions bounded by maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds. (3) A suspend write that a Postgres failover leaves ambiguous, which keeps the seal in place. Rejected because the next idle tick re-seals, re-checkpoints, and retries the write, and refused messages fall back to the DLQ under SPEC-5f.
USEFUL [Standing context Leader capacity., Per-message and idle costs., Refused-seal cost., Count queries.]: these four entries cover every rate the staging creates, so this lens needs only the HEAD diff plus a whole read to confirm that nothing new appears.

### [non-spec.53.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 2.1 **Lifetime** is one guarded `Update` with no Guarded-write repeat and no `TerminatedAt == at` landed identity; nil runs `notifySessionExpiry` and `recordCompleted`, while the sentinel, `ErrNotFound`, or a logged store error does nothing more for the row that tick — BECAUSE every replica runs `Tick` under the harness's one stepped clock, so a captured `at` is not unique and any repeat-based landed test double-fires the hook (C6.2, I7); with no repeat the writer conforms to D7 **When the hook does not run.** and the **Terminal write without its hook.** relaxation row unedited — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "earlier try returned a store error" conjunct (still double-fires: A's try errors unapplied, B applies with the same `at`, A's repeat sees `expired`/`TerminatedAt == at`); a per-write identity column (rejected by Settled **Idle atomicity.**); an exception clause in D7 and the relaxation row (keeps the non-unique repeat).
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce a repeat for any watchdog terminal writer keyed on a timestamp the sweep captured. `Watchdog.Tick(ctx, now)` takes `now` from its caller and the harness steps one clock for all replicas, so two replicas' sweeps write identical rows. A store error that did not apply is retried by the next tick's listing (5 s); one that applied takes the D7 relaxation like every other `funnel` watchdog writer. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go `func (w *Watchdog) Tick`; non-spec-changes.md Testing harness "under one stepped clock".
MISTAKE: an earlier round staged the Guarded-write repeat with a `TerminatedAt == at` landed identity on `sweepSuspended`; it contradicted D7 bullet 1 and the X4 relaxation row and double-fired under concurrent sweeps. Removed in round 53, with C6.2 rewritten to assert no hook, release, or backlog resume after a store error and expiry on a later tick.

### [non-spec.53.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: closed both G1 findings by deleting the `sweepSuspended` lifetime-write repeat (CODE-6 item 2.1) and its `TerminatedAt == at` landed identity. The sweep runs the two expiry hooks only when its one `Update` returns nil, and on the sentinel, `ErrNotFound`, or a logged store error it does nothing more for the row that tick. C6.2 is rewritten to match — BECAUSE this makes `sweepSuspended` behave like every other watchdog `funnel` writer, so D7 **When the hook does not run.** bullet 1 and the **Invariant relaxations.** row become true without an edit. It also means no landed identity is needed. A not-applied store error is retried by the next 5 s tick, which re-lists the still-`suspended` row. An applied-then-errored write leaves an `expired` row that the next listing skips, and that is the D7 relaxation already accepted for every terminal writer — ALTERNATIVES: (a) apply both reviewer fixes, an exception clause in D7 and the relaxation row plus a store-error conjunct on the identity. Rejected as hair, and it is still not unique under the shared stepped clock: A's try errors without applying, B's sweep applies with the same `at`, and A's repeat then sees `expired` with `TerminatedAt == at` after an earlier store error, so A runs the hook a second time. (b) A per-write identity column. Rejected by Settled **Idle atomicity.** (no new column, no timestamp identity).
WATCHOUT: no timestamp-based identity of a terminal write is unique in the expansive harness, because `Watchdog.Tick` takes `now` from its caller and one stepped clock drives every replica. Adding an "earlier store error" conjunct does not fix this when two replicas run the same guarded write: the erroring replica's write did not apply, and the peer's write did. `classifyCommit` escapes the problem only because the generation `G` and the sandbox identify the attempt — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:588; non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5 step 3 table.
CORRECTS [non-spec.53.review-reliability.1]: its WATCHOUT, "pair it with 'an earlier try returned a store error', as `classifyCommit` does", does not make a captured-timestamp identity unique when two replicas run the same guarded write under one clock (see the WATCHOUT above).
FACT: the repeat came in at 8a9b70e99 (open-decisions firing 2). The text before it logged a store error and let the next tick re-evaluate. The Design **Guarded-write repeat rule.** names only `classifyCommit` and `classifyIdleWrite` as callers, so deleting the `sweepSuspended` use leaves that rule and summary line 98 true — EVIDENCE: git show 8a9b70e99 non-spec-changes.md hunk at CODE-6 item 2.1.
